L   @moduledoc """
T 2 source.elixir
T 16 source.elixir comment.block.documentation.heredoc
L   A keyword list is a list that consists exclusively of two-element tuples.
T 75 source.elixir comment.block.documentation.heredoc
L 
L   The first element of these tuples is known as the *key*, and it must be an atom.
T 82 source.elixir comment.block.documentation.heredoc
L   The second element, known as the *value*, can be any term.
T 60 source.elixir comment.block.documentation.heredoc
L 
L   Keywords are mostly used to work with optional values. For a general introduction
T 83 source.elixir comment.block.documentation.heredoc
L   to keywords and how they compare with maps, see our [Keyword and Maps](keywords-and-maps.md)
T 94 source.elixir comment.block.documentation.heredoc
L   guide.
T 8 source.elixir comment.block.documentation.heredoc
L 
L   ## Examples
T 13 source.elixir comment.block.documentation.heredoc
L 
L   For example, the following is a keyword list:
T 47 source.elixir comment.block.documentation.heredoc
L 
L       [{:exit_on_close, true}, {:active, :once}, {:packet_size, 1024}]
T 70 source.elixir comment.block.documentation.heredoc
L 
L   Elixir provides a special and more concise syntax for keyword lists:
T 70 source.elixir comment.block.documentation.heredoc
L 
L       [exit_on_close: true, active: :once, packet_size: 1024]
T 61 source.elixir comment.block.documentation.heredoc
L 
L   The two syntaxes return the exact same value.
T 47 source.elixir comment.block.documentation.heredoc
L 
L   A *key* can be any atom, consisting of Unicode letters, numbers,
T 66 source.elixir comment.block.documentation.heredoc
L   an underscore or the `@` sign. If the *key* should have any other
T 67 source.elixir comment.block.documentation.heredoc
L   characters, such as spaces, you can wrap it in quotes:
T 56 source.elixir comment.block.documentation.heredoc
L 
L       iex> ["exit on close": true]
T 34 source.elixir comment.block.documentation.heredoc
L       ["exit on close": true]
T 29 source.elixir comment.block.documentation.heredoc
L 
L   Wrapping an atom in quotes does not make it a string. Keyword list
T 68 source.elixir comment.block.documentation.heredoc
L   *keys* are always atoms. Quotes should only be used when necessary
T 68 source.elixir comment.block.documentation.heredoc
L   or Elixir will issue a warning.
T 33 source.elixir comment.block.documentation.heredoc
L 
L   ## Duplicate keys and ordering
T 32 source.elixir comment.block.documentation.heredoc
L 
L   A keyword list may have duplicate keys so it is not strictly a key-value
T 74 source.elixir comment.block.documentation.heredoc
L   data type. However, most of the functions in this module work on a
T 68 source.elixir comment.block.documentation.heredoc
L   key-value structure and behave similar to the functions you would
T 67 source.elixir comment.block.documentation.heredoc
L   find in the `Map` module. For example, `Keyword.get/3` will get the first
T 75 source.elixir comment.block.documentation.heredoc
L   entry matching the given key, regardless if duplicate entries exist.
T 70 source.elixir comment.block.documentation.heredoc
L   Similarly, `Keyword.put/3` and `Keyword.delete/2` ensure all duplicate
T 72 source.elixir comment.block.documentation.heredoc
L   entries for a given key are removed when invoked. Note, however, that
T 71 source.elixir comment.block.documentation.heredoc
L   keyword list operations need to traverse the whole list in order to find
T 74 source.elixir comment.block.documentation.heredoc
L   keys, so these operations are slower than their map counterparts.
T 67 source.elixir comment.block.documentation.heredoc
L 
L   A handful of functions exist to handle duplicate keys, for example,
T 69 source.elixir comment.block.documentation.heredoc
L   `get_values/2` returns all values for a given key and `delete_first/2`
T 72 source.elixir comment.block.documentation.heredoc
L   deletes just the first entry of the existing ones.
T 52 source.elixir comment.block.documentation.heredoc
L 
L   Even though lists preserve the existing order, the functions in
T 65 source.elixir comment.block.documentation.heredoc
L   `Keyword` do not guarantee any ordering. For example, if you invoke
T 69 source.elixir comment.block.documentation.heredoc
L   `Keyword.put(opts, new_key, new_value)`, there is no guarantee for
T 68 source.elixir comment.block.documentation.heredoc
L   where `new_key` will be added to (the front, the end or anywhere else).
T 73 source.elixir comment.block.documentation.heredoc
L 
L   Given ordering is not guaranteed, it is not recommended to pattern
T 68 source.elixir comment.block.documentation.heredoc
L   match on keyword lists either. For example, a function such as:
T 65 source.elixir comment.block.documentation.heredoc
L 
L       def my_function([some_key: value, another_key: another_value])
T 68 source.elixir comment.block.documentation.heredoc
L 
L   will match
T 12 source.elixir comment.block.documentation.heredoc
L 
L       my_function([some_key: :foo, another_key: :bar])
T 54 source.elixir comment.block.documentation.heredoc
L 
L   but it won't match
T 20 source.elixir comment.block.documentation.heredoc
L 
L       my_function([another_key: :bar, some_key: :foo])
T 54 source.elixir comment.block.documentation.heredoc
L 
L   Most of the functions in this module work in linear time. This means
T 70 source.elixir comment.block.documentation.heredoc
L   that the time it takes to perform an operation grows at the same
T 66 source.elixir comment.block.documentation.heredoc
L   rate as the length of the list.
T 33 source.elixir comment.block.documentation.heredoc
L 
L   ## Call syntax
T 16 source.elixir comment.block.documentation.heredoc
L 
L   When keyword lists are passed as the last argument to a function,
T 67 source.elixir comment.block.documentation.heredoc
L   the square brackets around the keyword list can be omitted. For
T 65 source.elixir comment.block.documentation.heredoc
L   example, the keyword list syntax:
T 35 source.elixir comment.block.documentation.heredoc
L 
L       String.split("1-0", "-", [trim: true, parts: 2])
T 54 source.elixir comment.block.documentation.heredoc
L 
L   can be written without the enclosing brackets whenever it is the last
T 71 source.elixir comment.block.documentation.heredoc
L   argument of a function call:
T 30 source.elixir comment.block.documentation.heredoc
L 
L       String.split("1-0", "-", trim: true, parts: 2)
T 52 source.elixir comment.block.documentation.heredoc
L 
L   Since tuples, lists and maps are treated similarly to function
T 64 source.elixir comment.block.documentation.heredoc
L   arguments in Elixir syntax, this property is also available to them:
T 70 source.elixir comment.block.documentation.heredoc
L 
L       iex> {1, 2, foo: :bar}
T 28 source.elixir comment.block.documentation.heredoc
L       {1, 2, [{:foo, :bar}]}
T 28 source.elixir comment.block.documentation.heredoc
L 
L       iex> [1, 2, foo: :bar]
T 28 source.elixir comment.block.documentation.heredoc
L       [1, 2, {:foo, :bar}]
T 26 source.elixir comment.block.documentation.heredoc
L 
L       iex> %{1 => 2, foo: :bar}
T 31 source.elixir comment.block.documentation.heredoc
L       %{1 => 2, :foo => :bar}
T 29 source.elixir comment.block.documentation.heredoc
L 
L   """
T 5 source.elixir comment.block.documentation.heredoc

T 64 source.elixir comment.block.documentation.heredoc

T 39 source.elixir comment.block.documentation.heredoc

L   @doc """
T 2 source.elixir
T 10 source.elixir comment.block.documentation.heredoc
L   Ensures the given `keyword` has only the keys given in `values`.
T 66 source.elixir comment.block.documentation.heredoc
L 
L   The second argument must be a list of atoms, specifying
T 57 source.elixir comment.block.documentation.heredoc
L   a given key, or tuples specifying a key and a default value.
T 62 source.elixir comment.block.documentation.heredoc
L 
L   If the keyword list has only the given keys, it returns
T 57 source.elixir comment.block.documentation.heredoc
L   `{:ok, keyword}` with default values applied. Otherwise it
T 60 source.elixir comment.block.documentation.heredoc
L   returns `{:error, invalid_keys}` with invalid keys.
T 53 source.elixir comment.block.documentation.heredoc
L 
L   See also: `validate!/2`.
T 26 source.elixir comment.block.documentation.heredoc
L 
L   ## Examples
T 13 source.elixir comment.block.documentation.heredoc
L 
L       iex> {:ok, result} = Keyword.validate([], [one: 1, two: 2])
T 65 source.elixir comment.block.documentation.heredoc
L       iex> Enum.sort(result)
T 28 source.elixir comment.block.documentation.heredoc
L       [one: 1, two: 2]
T 22 source.elixir comment.block.documentation.heredoc
L 
L       iex> {:ok, result} = Keyword.validate([two: 3], [one: 1, two: 2])
T 71 source.elixir comment.block.documentation.heredoc
L       iex> Enum.sort(result)
T 28 source.elixir comment.block.documentation.heredoc
L       [one: 1, two: 3]
T 22 source.elixir comment.block.documentation.heredoc
L 
L   If atoms are given, they are supported as keys but do not
T 59 source.elixir comment.block.documentation.heredoc
L   provide a default value:
T 26 source.elixir comment.block.documentation.heredoc
L 
L       iex> {:ok, result} = Keyword.validate([], [:one, two: 2])
T 63 source.elixir comment.block.documentation.heredoc
L       iex> Enum.sort(result)
T 28 source.elixir comment.block.documentation.heredoc
L       [two: 2]
T 14 source.elixir comment.block.documentation.heredoc
L 
L       iex> {:ok, result} = Keyword.validate([one: 1], [:one, two: 2])
T 69 source.elixir comment.block.documentation.heredoc
L       iex> Enum.sort(result)
T 28 source.elixir comment.block.documentation.heredoc
L       [one: 1, two: 2]
T 22 source.elixir comment.block.documentation.heredoc
L 
L   Passing unknown keys returns an error:
T 40 source.elixir comment.block.documentation.heredoc
L 
L       iex> Keyword.validate([three: 3, four: 4], [one: 1, two: 2])
T 66 source.elixir comment.block.documentation.heredoc
L       {:error, [:four, :three]}
T 31 source.elixir comment.block.documentation.heredoc
L 
L   Passing the same key multiple times also errors:
T 50 source.elixir comment.block.documentation.heredoc
L 
L       iex> Keyword.validate([one: 1, two: 2, one: 1], [:one, :two])
T 67 source.elixir comment.block.documentation.heredoc
L       {:error, [:one]}
T 22 source.elixir comment.block.documentation.heredoc
L

T 10 source.elixir comment.block.documentation.heredoc
L   Similar to `validate/2` but returns the keyword or raises an error.
T 69 source.elixir comment.block.documentation.heredoc
L 
L   ## Examples
T 13 source.elixir comment.block.documentation.heredoc
L 
L       iex> Keyword.validate!([], [one: 1, two: 2]) |> Enum.sort()
T 65 source.elixir comment.block.documentation.heredoc
L       [one: 1, two: 2]
T 22 source.elixir comment.block.documentation.heredoc
L       iex> Keyword.validate!([two: 3], [one: 1, two: 2]) |> Enum.sort()
T 71 source.elixir comment.block.documentation.heredoc
L       [one: 1, two: 3]
T 22 source.elixir comment.block.documentation.heredoc
L 
L   If atoms are given, they are supported as keys but do not
T 59 source.elixir comment.block.documentation.heredoc
L   provide a default value:
T 26 source.elixir comment.block.documentation.heredoc
L 
L       iex> Keyword.validate!([], [:one, two: 2]) |> Enum.sort()
T 63 source.elixir comment.block.documentation.heredoc
L       [two: 2]
T 14 source.elixir comment.block.documentation.heredoc
L       iex> Keyword.validate!([one: 1], [:one, two: 2]) |> Enum.sort()
T 69 source.elixir comment.block.documentation.heredoc
L       [one: 1, two: 2]
T 22 source.elixir comment.block.documentation.heredoc
L 
L   Passing unknown keys raises an error:
T 39 source.elixir comment.block.documentation.heredoc
L 
L       iex> Keyword.validate!([three: 3], [one: 1, two: 2])
T 58 source.elixir comment.block.documentation.heredoc
L       ** (ArgumentError) unknown keys [:three] in [three: 3], the allowed keys are: [:one, :two]
T 96 source.elixir comment.block.documentation.heredoc
L 
L   Passing the same key multiple times also errors:
T 50 source.elixir comment.block.documentation.heredoc
L 
L       iex> Keyword.validate!([one: 1, two: 2, one: 1], [:one, :two])
T 68 source.elixir comment.block.documentation.heredoc

L   @doc """
T 2 source.elixir
T 10 source.elixir comment.block.documentation.heredoc
L   Gets the value for `key` and updates it in one pass, deleting duplicate keys,
T 79 source.elixir comment.block.documentation.heredoc
L   raising if `key` can't be found in `keywords`.
T 48 source.elixir comment.block.documentation.heredoc
L 
L   The `fun` argument receives the value under `key` and must return a
T 69 source.elixir comment.block.documentation.heredoc
L   two-element tuple: the current value (the retrieved value, which can be
T 73 source.elixir comment.block.documentation.heredoc
L   operated on before being returned) and the new value to be stored under
T 73 source.elixir comment.block.documentation.heredoc
L   `key`.
T 8 source.elixir comment.block.documentation.heredoc
L 
L   Returns a tuple that contains the current value returned by
T 61 source.elixir comment.block.documentation.heredoc
L   `fun` and a new keyword list with the updated value under `key`.
T 66 source.elixir comment.block.documentation.heredoc
L 
L   ## Examples
T 13 source.elixir comment.block.documentation.heredoc
L 
L       iex> Keyword.get_and_update!([a: 1], :a, fn current_value ->
T 66 source.elixir comment.block.documentation.heredoc
L       ...>   {current_value, "new value!"}
T 42 source.elixir comment.block.documentation.heredoc
L       ...> end)
T 15 source.elixir comment.block.documentation.heredoc
L       {1, [a: "new value!"]}
T 28 source.elixir comment.block.documentation.heredoc

T 2 source.elixir
T 10 source.elixir comment.block.documentation.heredoc

T 78 source.elixir comment.block.documentation.heredoc

T 8 source.elixir comment.block.documentation.heredoc
L 
L   The provided `fun` receives each `{key, value}` pair in the `keywords` as its only
T 84 source.elixir comment.block.documentation.heredoc
L   argument. Returns a tuple with the first keyword list containing all the
T 74 source.elixir comment.block.documentation.heredoc
L   elements in `keywords` for which applying `fun` returned a truthy value, and
T 78 source.elixir comment.block.documentation.heredoc

T 79 source.elixir comment.block.documentation.heredoc

T 35 source.elixir comment.block.documentation.heredoc

T 13 source.elixir comment.block.documentation.heredoc
